***********************{Explanation:
The given parabola is y = (x - 1)^2 + 5.
To find its axis, focus, and vertex, we need to rewrite it in the standard form y = a(x - h)^2 + k.

Comparing with this standard form, we have:
a = 1, h = 1, and k = 5.
The vertex of the parabola is (h, k) = (1, 5).

The axis of symmetry is the vertical line x = h, which in this case is x = 1.

Now, to find the focus, we can use the formula:
Focus = (h ± 1/4a, k)
Since a = 1, we have
Focus = (h ± 1/4, k) = (1 + 0.25, 5) or (1 - 0.25, 5)
Therefore, the focus is (1 + 0.25, 5) which equals (1.25, 5).

Thus,
Final Answer: The vertex of the parabola is at point (1, 5), the axis of symmetry is x = 1 and the focus is (1.25, 5).}

***********************{Explanation: 
To solve for a parabola passing through points (2,8), (-1,3), and (0,4), we need to find a general equation for a parabola which is y = ax^2 + bx + c.

Given points are:
- P1(2, 8) => 8 = a*2^2 + b*2 + c 
  => 8 = 4a + 2b + c
- P2(-1,3) => 3 = a*(-1)^2 + b*(-1) + c 
  => 3 = a - b + c
- P3(0,4) => 4 = a*0^2 + b*0 + c 
  => 4 = c

From the point (0,4), we have the equation: 
c = 4. We will substitute this value of c in other equations.

Substituting c = 4 in first equation (8 = 4a + 2b + c):
=> 8 = 4a + 2b + 4
=> 4a + 2b = -8

Now, substituting the value of c = 4 and above equation in third equation (3 = a - b + c):
=> 3 = a - b + 4
=> a - b = -1

We have two equations with two unknowns: 
- a - b = -1   => Equation 1
- 4a + 2b = -8  => Equation 2

Multiplying equation 1 by 2 to make coefficients of 'b' equal in both the equations:
=> 2a - 2b = -2  => Multiplied Equation 1

Adding the multiplied equation with the second equation: 
(4a + 2b) + (2a - 2b) = -8 + (-2)
=> 6a = -10
=> a = -5/3

Now, substitute the value of a into Equation 1 to solve for b:
 => -5/3 - b = -1
=> -b = -1 + 5/3
=> -b = (-3+5)/3 
=>-b = 2/3
=> b = -2/3

Substitute the values of a and b into one of the original equations, say y = ax^2 + bx + c:
y = -5/3 * x^2 - 2/3x + 4

The equation for the parabola passing through the given points is: 
y = (-5/3) * x^2 - (2/3)x + 4.
}

{Final Answer: "(-5/3)x^2 - (2/3)x + 4"}

Explanation:

To find the equation of a circle, we need to use the standard form of the equation of a circle, which is (x-h)^2 + (y-k)^2 = r^2.

Here, (-2,1) and (6,-3) are two points on the circle. We can use these points to find the center (h,k) and radius r of the circle.

Let's calculate the midpoint of the line segment joining the two points:

Midpoint = ((-2+6)/2, (1+(-3))/2) = (2,-1)

This midpoint is the center (h,k) of the circle. Now we need to find the distance between this center and either point (this will be our radius). 

The distance formula is √((x2 - x1)^2 + (y2 - y1)^2).

Plugging in values, we get: r = √((-2-2)^2 + (1+1)^2) = √(16+4) = √20.

Now that we have the center and radius, we can plug these into our circle equation formula:

(x-2)^2 + (y+1)^2 = (√20)^2

This simplifies to: x^2 - 4*x + 4 + y^2 + 2*y + 1 = 20

Which further simplifies to: x^2 + y^2 - 4*x + 2*y -12 = 0

***********************Here's the solution to the problem:

**Problem:** X^2 + y^2 + 6x - 4y - 12 = 0

**Step 1: Rearrange the equation to get it in the standard form of a circle**

The given equation is not in the standard form, so we need to rearrange it. We can do this by completing the square for both x and y terms.

X^2 + 6x + y^2 - 4y = 12

**Step 2: Complete the square for x and y**

To complete the square for x, we add (6/2)^2 = 9 to both sides of the equation. Similarly, to complete the square for y, we add (-4/2)^2 = 4 to both sides.

(X^2 + 6x + 9) - 9 + (y^2 - 4y + 4) - 4 = 12

**Step 3: Simplify the equation**

Now we can simplify the equation by combining like terms.

(X+3)^2 - 9 + (y-2)^2 - 4 = 12

(X+3)^2 + (y-2)^2 = 12 + 9 + 4

**Step 4: Write the equation in standard form**

The standard form of a circle is (x-h)^2 + (y-k)^2 = r^2, where (h,k) is the center and r is the radius.

(X+3)^2 + (y-2)^2 = 25

**Step 5: Identify the center and radius**

Comparing this equation with the standard form, we can see that:

Center = (-3, 2)
Radius = √25 = 5

Therefore, the final answer is:

}

***********************{Final Answer: "[(-4, -1), (5, 8)]"} 

Explanation:

To find the intersection points of the line y = x + 3 and the circle x^2 + y^2 = 17, we can substitute the expression for y from the line equation into the circle equation.

The line equation is y = x + 3. We will substitute this value of y in place of y in the circle equation:

x^2 + (x+3)^2 = 17

Simplify and expand the equation:

x^2 + x^2 + 6x + 9 = 17
Combine like terms:
2x^2 + 6x - 8 = 0 

Now, we have a quadratic equation in standard form. We can solve for x using the quadratic formula: x = (-b ± √(b² - 4ac)) / 2a.

For our equation, a = 2, b = 6, and c = -8.

x = (-(6) ± √((6)^2 - 4(2)(-8))) / (2(2))
x = (-6 ± √(36 + 64)) / 4
x = (-6 ± √100) / 4

Simplify:

x = (-6 ± 10) / 4

We now have two possible values for x, depending on whether we use the positive or negative sign in front of the square root.

x1 = (-6 + 10) / 4
x2 = (-6 - 10) / 4

For x1:

x1 = (4) / 4 
x1 = 1 

For x2:
 x2 = (-16)/4 
x2 = -4


Now, we can find the corresponding y-values by plugging these x values back into the line equation y = x + 3.

For x1:

y1 = (1) + 3
y1 = 4 


For x2:

y2 = (-4) + 3 
y2 = -1


Therefore, the two intersection points are: (x1, y1), which is (1, 4) and (x2, y2), which is (-4, -1).

***********************{Explanation:
Step 1: Find the Center and Radius
The equation of a circle can be written as (x-h)^2 + (y-k)^2 = r^2, where (h,k) is the center of the circle and r is its radius. To find these values, we need to solve for the center and radius.

Step 2: Calculate the Midpoints
To calculate the center, we can use the midpoints of the line segments connecting each pair of points.
Midpoint of (1,1) and (2,-3) = ((1+2)/2 , (1-3)/2) = (3/2, -1)
Midpoint of (2,-3) and (5,-1) = ((2+5)/2 , (-3+-1)/2) = (7/2, -2)

Step 3: The line joining these midpoints will be perpendicular to the radius passing through each point. So the center lies on this line.
Slope of the line segment from (1,1) to (2,-3) is (y2-y1)/(x2-x1)=(-3-1)/(2-1)= -4
The slope of a perpendicular line will be 1/4.

Now using midpoint (7/2, -2), and point (5,-1) we can find the slope between them which is (-2+1)/(7/2-5) = -1/(7/2 - 10/2) = -1/-3 = 1/3

Step 4: Since this line has a slope of 1/3 and it passes through point (3/2, -1), we can use the point-slope form to find its equation.
The slope is given as m = 1/3. 
So y+1= 1/3(x-3/2)
y + 1 = 1/3x - 1/2
Multiplying both sides by 6, we get:
6(y + 1) = 2(x - 3/2)
6y + 6 = 2x - 3
8 = 2x - 6y
x = 7/2 - 3y

Step 5: Substituting this equation into one of the equations for a circle, we get:
(7/2 - 3y)^2 + (y+1)^2 = r^2


Step 6: Using any point on the line from step 4 and plugging it in to solve for radius squared (r^2) using equation found by putting y=1 from step 5.
Plugging y=1 into the circle equation ((7/2 - 3y)^2 + (y+1)^2), we get:
((7/2-3)^2+(1+1)^2)=
((7/2-3)^2+(2)^2)= 
((7/2-6)^2+(2)^2) = 
((7/2 - 12/2)^2 + (2)^2) =
((-5/2)^2 + (2)^2)
=25/4+4
=(25+16)/4=41/4

Step 7: Write down the final answer.
{Final Answer: "((x-7/2)+3y)^2+(y-1)^2 = 41/4"}
This equation represents a circle with center at (7/2, 1) and radius r = √(41/4)=√41/2.

***********************{ "Explanation": "To find the equation of an ellipse, we need to know its major and minor axes. Let's consider the given points: (0, 7) and (0, -7), which are on the y-axis and have a distance of 14 units between them. This indicates that the major axis is along the y-axis, with length 2a = 14. Thus, a = 7.

Similarly, considering the foci at (0, 5) and (0, -5), we find their distance from the center to be c = 5.

Now, to determine the equation of this ellipse, we use the formula for an ellipse with its major axis along the y-axis: ((y-k)^2)/a^2 + ((x-h)^2)/b^2 = 1

Here, h and k represent the coordinates of the center. Since our foci lie on the y-axis, the center must also be on the y-axis. Therefore, (h, k) = (0, 0).

To find b, we can use the relationship c^2 = a^2 - b^2 from ellipse properties.

Plugging in the values for a and c: c = 5 and a = 7

5^2 = 7^2 - b^2
25 = 49 - b^2
b^2 = 24
b = sqrt(24) 

The equation of the ellipse is then ((y-0)^2)/7^2 + ((x-0)^2)/(sqrt(24))^2 = 1

Simplifying this, we get:

(y^2)/49 + (x^2)/24 = 1" }

4x^2/36 - 9y^2/36 = 36/36
This simplifies to (x^2)/9 - (y^2)/4 = 1.

Now, to convert this equation to the standard form of a hyperbola, we need to complete the square. However, since the equation is already in the desired form, we can directly identify the values of a and b.
a^2 = 9
b^2 = 4

So, a = √9 = 3 and b = √4 = 2.

The standard form of a hyperbola with a transverse axis along x-axis is (y/k)^2 - (x/h)^2 = 1. Comparing this with the given equation, we get k^2 = a^2 = 9 and h^2 = b^2 = 4.
Therefore, k = √9 = 3 and h = √4 = 2.

Thus, the standard form of the hyperbola is (y/3)^2 - (x/2)^2 = 1.}

***********************{Final Answer: "y = -(x^2/4) + 3 - (12/4)"}
Explanation:
First, let's rearrange the equation to isolate y:

x^2 - 4x + 8y + 12 = 0
8y = -x^2 + 4x - 12
y = (-x^2 + 4x - 12)/8

Now, we need to complete the square for x:

-x^2 + 4x - 12 = -(x^2 - 4x) - 12
= -(x^2 - 4x + 4) + 4 - 12 (add and subtract 4 to complete the square)
= -(x - 2)^2 + (-12/1)

Now, plug this back into the equation for y:

y = (-x^2 + 4x - 12)/8
= -((x-2)^2) + (-12/1)/8
= -((x-2)^2)/8 + (-3/2)

Combine like terms and simplify:

y = -((x-2)^2)/8 + (-3/2)
= -(x^2/4) + 3 - (12/4)
= -(x^2/4) + 3 - 3
= -(x^2/4) + 3 - (12/4)
= -(x^2/4) + 3 - 3

"Explanation": "एक परबोला का समीकरण लिखने से पहले, हमें यह जानना होगा कि कैसे इस तरह की समस्याओं का समाधान किया जाए। \n1. दाईं ओर खुलते हुए एक परबोला के लिए, उसका पार्श्वीय अक्ष x-युग्म (y = k(x - h)^2 + c) में वर्णित किया जाता है। ध्यान दें, 'd' वास्तव में 1/a है जहां a एक गुणक है, लेकिन यहाँ हम इसे केवल 'd' के रूप में उपयोग कर रहे हैं। \n2. परबोला (-4, 1) वर्टेक्स पर खुलती है। वर्टेक्स का समीकरण y = a(x - h)^2 + k है, जहां (h, k) हमारे मामले में (-4, 1) है। जैसा कि हमने पहले देखा, 'a' और 'd' एक-दूसरे के व्युत्क्रम हैं और पूरक हैं। \n3. ध्यान केंद्र (-2, 1) पर होता है। ध्यान केंद्र इस तरह से परिभाषित किया जाता है: (h + d/2, k + c), जहां (h, k) वर्टेक्स पर हैं। इस मामले में, हमारा समीकरण y = a(x - (-4))^2 + 1 और ध्यान केंद्र (-2, 1) है, जिससे हम 'a' की गणना कर सकते हैं और फिर 'c'। \n4. एक बार जब आप अपने वर्ग (x - h)^2 में a की निगरानी करते हैं, तो यह आपको इस समस्या से बचने में मदद करेगा। ध्यान दें, समीकरणों के लिए एक केंद्र और शीर्ष जोड़ा गया है, इसलिए उनके बीच का अंतर (a - 0) सीधे एक परबोला की गुणवत्ता में आ जाता है। \n5. एक बार जब आप अपने वर्ग (x - h)^2 में a की निगरानी करते हैं, तो यह आपको इस समस्या से बचने में मदद करेगा। ध्यान दें, समीकरणों के लिए एक केंद्र और शीर्ष जोड़ा गया है, इसलिए उनके बीच का अंतर (a - 0) सीधे एक परबोला की गुणवत्ता में आ जाता है। \n6. समीकरण को याद रखें, क्योंकि यह पार्श्वीय अक्ष x-युग्म (y = k(x - h)^2 + c) में वर्णित करता है। ध्यान दें, 'd' वास्तव में 1/a है जहां a एक गुणक है, लेकिन यहाँ हम इसे केवल 'd' के रूप में उपयोग कर रहे हैं। \n7. समीकरण याद रखें, क्योंकि यह पार्श्वीय अक्ष x-युग्म (y = k(x - ***********************हाय! मैं आपकी सहायता करने के लिए यहाँ हूँ।


मैं पहले त्वरित स्थापनाओं को समझता हूँ:


1.  **लाइन y = x + 3:** ये एक रेखा की समीकरण है जिसकी दिशा (1, 1) पर 45-60 डिग्री वाले चौराहे से गुजरती है।


2.  **सर्कल x^2 + y^2 = 17:** ये एक सर्कल का समीकरण है जिसका केंद्र (0, 0) और कोणीय पैरामीटर r हैं। 


मैं क्योंकि तृतीय शिखर के बिंदु ज्ञात करती हूँ!


सर्कल के विशिष्ट स्थान में, निम्नलिखित तरीका है:


1.  लाइन और सर्कल के बीच अंतराल को खोजने के लिए, सर्कल का समीकरण x^2 + y^2 = r^2 है।


2.  रेखा में 'a' और 'b' की गणना करने के लिए सामान्य रूप में x^2 + (y - b)^2 = a^2 को रखें।


3.  एक बार जब आप 'a' और 'b' निकालते हैं, तो अंतराल की दिशा 'a*x + b*y' = c समीकरण द्वारा गणितीय रूप में निर्धारित करें, जहाँ c व्युत्क्रम पर 0 से अलग नहीं होता। 


4.  इसके बाद, अंतराल की कोशिश करने के लिए x^2 + y^2 = r^2 समीकरण का उपयोग करें।


5.  अंत में, अंतराल के चौराहे पर सर्कल और रेखा को खोजने के लिए 'a*x + b*y' = c समीकरण की निम्नतम श्रृंखला का उपयोग करके सर्कल के स्थान पर विस्तृत अंतराल का मूल्यांकन करें और अपने गणितीय समाधान को प्रस्तुत करें। 


जैसा कि ऊपर उल्लिखित था, लाइन y = x + 3 और सर्कल x^2 + y^2 = 17 ज्ञात करती है:


1.  स्थापनाओं में रेखा के समीकरण में एक बिंदु, जिस पर ये होती है, (0, -3)। 


2.  सर्कल के विशिष्ट मामलों में, यह खोजने के लिए हम अंतराल समीकरण को रेखा के समीकरण के साथ जोड़ते हैं:


x^2 + y^2 = 17


य या x + 3 = 
य
स्थापनाओं को पुनः व्यवस्थित करना: 


\begin{align*}
x^2+\left(x+3\right)^2&amp;=17\\
\Rightarrow\qquad 2x^2+6x+9&amp;=17\\
\Rightarrow\qquad 2x^2+6x-8&amp;=0\\
\Rightarrow\qquad x^2+3x-4&amp;=0
\end{align*} 


इसे हल करने के लिए, मैं फैक्टरिंग का उपयोग करती हूँ:


x^2 + 3*x - 4 = (x - 1)(x + 4) = 0


अपने दो विभाजित स्थानों पर शून्य और अंतराल समीकरण की तुलना करने के लिए, मैं निम्नलिखित तरीका है:


\begin{align*}
(x-1)(x+4)&amp;=0\\
\Rightarrow\qquad x^2 + 3x - 4 &amp; = 0\\
&amp;\text {स्थापनाओं की तुलना करने के लिए सर्कल और रेखा अंतराल}
\end{align*}


मैं सोचता हूँ कि यह एक बिंदु है जहाँ हम अपने समाधान को प्रस्तुत करें:


अपने गणितीय समाधान में, (x - 1)(x + 4) = 0 और x^2 + y^2 = r^2 अंतराल समीकरणों में दोनों विशिष्ट स्थापनाएं प्रस्तुत करती हैं:


\begin{align*}
(x-1)(x+4)&amp;=0\\
&amp;\text {स्थापनाओं के लिए सर्कल और रेखा अंतराल}\\
x&amp;= 1\\ 
x + 4 &amp; = 0 \\
&amp;\Rightarrow x=-4
\end{align*}


अंत में, मैं खोजने के लिए समीकरणों को रेखा के साथ जोड़ती हूँ:


1.  स्थापनाओं में रेखा के समीकरण में एक बिंदु, जिस पर ये होती है, (-4, -1)।


2.  सर्कल के विशिष्ट मामलों में, यह खोजने के लिए हम अंतराल समीकरण को रेखा के समीकरण के साथ जोड़ते हैं:


x^2 + y^2 = 17


य या x + 3 = 
य
मुझे लगता है कि अब मैं अपनी समस्या के समाधान को प्रस्तुत कर सकती हूँ:


मेरा गणितीय समाधान यह है:


सर्कल और रेखा के चौराहों पर बिंदु (-4, -1) और (1, 2) हैं.


इसलिए, मेरा अंतिम उत्तर यह होगा:


{Final Answer: "(-4,-1),(1,2)"}

तो क्या आप खुशी से जाते हैं, अब आपके प्रश्नों के लिए!

***********************{Explanation: The given points lie on the circle, so we can use them to form equations based on their distance from a point (h,k) as per the formula for a circle which is (x-h)^2 + (y-k)^2 = r^2 where (h,k) is the center and r is radius.
To find the equation of the circle passing through points (1, 1), (2, -3), and (5, -1), let's first determine the center of the circle by finding the point that satisfies all three equations. This can be done using the formula for the circumcenter of a triangle which is ((a*x1^2 + b*x2^2 + c*x3^2) / (a+b+c) , (a*y1^2 + b*y2^2 + c*y3^2)/(a+b+c)).
where (x1, y1), (x2, y2), and (x3, y3) are the given points and a=1, b=-3, c=-1.

Plugging in values for the points we get: 
((1*1^2 + -3*2^2 + -1*5^2)/(1-3-1)) , ((1*1^2 + -3*-3^2 + -1*-1^2)/(1-3-1))
(-1*(-7)/-5) , (-1*(10)/-5)
7/5 = -7/5 
This doesn't give us a point that satisfies all three equations as per our formula. We'll solve the system of equation of circle by solving for circumcircle (xh)^2 + (yk)^2 = r^2.

We know that the equation of any point on this circle is x^2-6y-y+1=0.
(x-3)^2+(y-(-4))^2=r^2
Therefore, a = 1, b=-3 and c = -1 in the formula for circumcenter.



Now let's find the point by solving (xh)^2 + (yk)^2 = r^2 with points (1, 1), (2, -3) and then use it to get the third point (5,-1).

***********************{ "Explanation": "Given parameters for ellipse: center (0,0), horizontal major axis length = 10, minor axis length = 6.

The standard equation of an ellipse is ((x - h)^2/a^2) + ((y - k)^2/b^2) = 1, where (h,k) is the center of the ellipse and a and b are lengths of semi-major and semi-minor axes respectively.

For horizontal major axis, the general form becomes: 
((x-0)^2)/((10/2)^2) + ((y-0)^2)/((6/2)^2) = 1

Simplifying: 
(x^2)/(25) + (y^2)/(9) = 1
Cross-multiplying:
9x^2 + 25y^2 = 225

The final equation of ellipse with given parameters is: 
9x^2 + 25y^2 = 225"

Explanation:
The given equation is x^2/4 + y^2/9 = 1, which is a standard form of an ellipse.
To find the area of the ellipse, we need to first find its major and minor axes.

In the equation x^2/4 + y^2/9 = 1, 
The denominator under x^2 is 4, and the denominator under y^2 is 9. These denominators represent the squares of the semi-major and semi-minor axis respectively.
Therefore, the length of major axis = √9 = 3 units
and the length of minor axis = √4 = 2 units

The area of an ellipse is given by the formula: A = π * a * b 
where a is the semi-major axis and b is the semi-minor axis.

Substituting the values, we get:
A = π * 3 * 2
A = 6π 

We are asked to provide the final answer as a numeric value. So we'll approximate π as 3.14.
Then, A ≈ 6 * 3.14 
The Final Answer is: {Final Answer: "18.84"}

***********************A nice problem!

Let's break it down step by step.

We are given a hyperbola with foci at (±6, 0) and vertices at (±4, 0). We need to find the equation of this hyperbola.

**Step 1: Identify the center**

Since the foci lie on the x-axis and have equal coordinates, the center of the hyperbola is at the origin, i.e., (0, 0).

**Step 2: Determine the value of c**

The distance between each focus and the center is 'c', which is 6 in this case.

**Step 3: Determine the value of a**

The distance between each vertex and the center is 'a', which is 4 in this case.

**Step 4: Calculate the value of b**

For a hyperbola with foci at (±c, 0), vertices at (±a, 0), we can use the relationship:
`c^2 = a^2 + b^2`

Substituting the values, we get:
`6^2 = 4^2 + b^2`
`36 = 16 + b^2`
`b^2 = 20`
`b = √20`

Simplifying the square root, we get:
`b = 2√5`

**Step 5: Write the equation of the hyperbola**

The standard form of a hyperbola with center (0, 0), foci at (±c, 0), and vertices at (±a, 0) is:
`x^2/a^2 - y^2/b^2 = 1`

Substituting the values of 'a' and 'b', we get:
`x^2/16 - y^2/(4√5) = 1`

**Final Answer:**

***********************I'm happy to help you solve the problem. Here's my step-by-step approach:

**Step 1:** The given information is that we have a hyperbola with its center at point (2,3), transverse axis length of 6 units, and conjugate axis length of 8 units.

To write the equation of the hyperbola, we need to determine the values of `a`, `b`, and `h` (the x-coordinate of the center).

**Step 2:** The given lengths are for the transverse and conjugate axes. Let's denote them as:

* Transverse axis length: 2a = 6 (since it's the distance between the vertices)
* Conjugate axis length: 2b = 8

Solving these equations, we get:

```python
a = 3
b = 4
```

These values represent half the lengths of the transverse and conjugate axes, respectively.

**Step 3:** Now that we have `a` and `b`, we can write the standard form equation of a hyperbola with its center at `(h, k)`:

( x - h )^2 / a^2 - ( y - k )^2 / b^2 = 1

Given that the center is at point (2,3), we know `h = 2` and `k = 3`. Plugging in these values along with our previously determined `a` and `b`, we get:

( x - 2)^2 / 9 - ( y - 3 )^2 / 16 = 1

**Final Answer:**

***********************{Explanation: The equation x^2/4 - y^2/9 = 1 represents a hyperbola.
First, we need to identify the values of 'a' and 'b' in the equation. In this case, a^2 = 4 and b^2 = 9.
Now, let's find the vertices:
The vertex form of a hyperbola is (h,k) ± (a,0). Here, h and k are zero since there's no x or y term in front of x or y squared respectively. In this case the vertices will be at (±4,0).
Next, we need to find the foci:
The formula for finding foci is c = sqrt(a^2 + b^2) where 'c' is the distance between the center and a focus.
Here, c = sqrt(9+4) = sqrt(13). Since c = sqrt(a^2+b^2), the coordinates of the foci will be at (±sqrt(13),0).
Now let's find the asymptotes:
To get the equation of the asymptotes, we divide each term in the original equation by 'c'. We divide x^2/4 by sqrt(13) and y^2/9 by sqrt(13). This gives us y = ±x*sqrt(4/9)*1/sqrt(13) which simplifies to y = ±x*(2/3)*1/sqrt(13).
Therefore, the final answer is:
{Final Answer: "(±4,0), (±sqrt(13),0), and y=±((2/3)/sqrt(13))x"}
}

***********************{
  "Explanation": "To find the equation of a hyperbola that satisfies the given conditions, we need to follow these steps:",
  "Step 1": "Determine the center and foci of the hyperbola.",
  "Step 2": "Calculate the values of a^2 and b^2 using the foci coordinates.",
  "Step 3": "Write the equation of the hyperbola in standard form."
}

Given:
foci: (0, ±√10)
Passing through: (2, 3)

Let's follow the steps:

**Step 1: Determine the center and foci**

From the given information, we can see that the foci are at (0, ±√10). This implies that the center of the hyperbola is at (0, 0).

**Step 2: Calculate a^2 and b^2**

The distance between each focus and the center is c = √10. Using the formula for the foci coordinates:

c^2 = a^2 + b^2

We can find the values of a^2 and b^2.

Let's assume that the hyperbola passes through the point (2, 3). We can use this information to set up an equation using the standard form of a hyperbola:

( x - h ) ^2 / a ^2 - ( y - k ) ^2 / b ^2 = 1

where (h, k) is the center of the hyperbola.

Substituting the values of h = 0, k = 0, and the point (2, 3), we get:

(2-0)^2/a^2 - (3-0)^2/b^2 = 1
4/a^2 - 9/b^2 = 1

We know that c^2 = a^2 + b^2. Substituting the value of c^2, we get:

10 = a^2 + b^2
a^2 = 3 (approx.)
b^2 = 7 (approx.)

Now we have the values of a^2 and b^2.

**Step 3: Write the equation of the hyperbola**

Using the standard form of a hyperbola, we can write its equation:

( x - h ) ^2 / a ^2 - ( y - k ) ^2 / b ^2 = 1
x^2/a^2 - y^2/b^2 = 1

Substituting the values of a^2 and b^2, we get:

x^2/3 - y^2/7 = 1

Therefore, the equation of the hyperbola that satisfies the given conditions is:

{x^2/3 - y^2/7 = 1}

So, 
{Final Answer: "x^2/3 - y^2/7 = 1"}

***********************{Explanation:
Step 1: The equation of a parabola can be written in the form y = a(x-h)^2 + k, where (h,k) is the vertex of the parabola.

Step 2: We are given that the parabola has its vertex at (3,4), so h=3 and k=4. This gives us the equation y = a(x-3)^2 + 4.

Step 3: The directrix y = 2 is perpendicular to the axis of symmetry of the parabola, which means it's parallel to the line x = 3. Therefore, the focus of the parabola lies on this line. The distance between the focus and the vertex (which is also the distance between the focus and the directrix) is equal to 1/(4a).

Step 4: Since the focus lies on the line x=3 and we know that a=(1/(2*distance)), plugging in the values we get, a = (1/2)*((x-3)^2 + 4 - 2).

Solving for a gives us a = ((x-3)^2)/2 + 1. Now we substitute this value back into our original equation y = a(x-3)^2 + 4 to find the final answer.
}

x^2 - 4x = -8y - 12
 
Now, to complete the square, we'll add (√(-4) / 2)^2 to both sides. We can rewrite √(-4) as 2i√(1), where i is the imaginary unit (the square root of -1). So, we have:
 
x^2 - 4x + (2i√1 / 2)^2 = -8y - 12
 
Simplifying further, we get:
 
x^2 - 4x + 4(i^2) = -8y - 12
 
Here, i^2 is equal to -1, so we have:
 
x^2 - 4x + 4(-1) = -8y - 12
 
Combining like terms on the left-hand side gives us:
 
x^2 - 4x - 4 = -8y - 12
 
The vertex form of a parabola is given by y = a(x-h)^2 + k, where (h,k) represents the coordinates of the vertex. To convert our equation into this format, let's rewrite it:
 
-8y = x^2 - 4x - 4
 
Now, divide both sides by -8 to isolate y:
 
y = -(1/8)x^2 + (1/2)x + 0.5
 
Therefore, the vertex form of the given equation is y = -(1/8)x^2 + (1/2)x + 0.5.

(8 = a*(2)^2 + b*2 + c). This simplifies to (8 = 4a + 2b + c).


Step 4:
Now, let's substitute point (-1,3) in the parabola equation:
(3 = a*(-1)^2 + b*(-1) + c), which simplifies to (3 = a - b + c).

(4 = a*0^2 + b*0 + c), which reduces to just (c = 4).

In step 4, it leaves us with the equation (3 = a - b + 4), which reduces to just (a - b = -1).

Step 11:

***********************{
  "Problem Explanation": "The problem requires finding the equation of a hyperbola that satisfies certain conditions.",
  "Given Conditions":
    {"foci": "(0,±√10)"},
    {"passes through": "(2,3)"}.
  "Solution Strategy": "To solve this problem, we need to use the properties of a hyperbola. We can start by finding the equation of the hyperbola in standard form, which is (x²/a²) - (y²/b²) = 1. Then, we can use the coordinates of the foci to find the values of 'a' and 'c'. Finally, we can substitute the point (2,3) into the equation to solve for 'b'."
}
 
First, let's recall that the standard form of a hyperbola centered at (0, 0) is given by:

(x²/a²) - (y²/b²) = 1

The foci of the hyperbola are located on the y-axis and have coordinates (0, ±√10). The distance between each focus and the center of the hyperbola is equal to 'c'. Since we know that c^2=a^2+b^2, where a and b are the lengths of the semi-major and semi-minor axes respectively.

Given that foci lies on y-axis so it can be written as (0±√10). Hence,
√10 = √(a² + b²)

Now we know that hyperbola passes through point (2,3).

To find the equation of the hyperbola, we need to substitute the coordinates of the point into the standard form and solve for 'b'.

Substituting x = 2, y = 3 in the standard form:

(2²/a²) - (3²/b²) = 1

Simplifying:

4/a² - 9/b² = 1

Now we know that √10 = √(a² + b²)

Squaring both sides to eliminate square root, and then multiplying by a² on both sides:

10(a² + b²) = (a² + b²)(a² + b²)
10a²+10b²=a^4+b^4
Rearranging the terms:
a^4-b^4-10a^2-10b^2=0

Factorizing a^4-b^4:
(a^2-b^2)(a^2+b^2)-10a^2-10b^2=0
 
Now factorize a^2-b^2 and a^2+b^2 
(a^2-b^2)((a^2+b^2)-10)=0
Since (a^2-b^2) is always positive as it’s the difference of squares,
So we will have  (a^2+b^2-10)=0
 
Solving for a² + b²:
 
a^2+b^2=10
 
Now going back to equation

4/a² - 9/b² = 1
Multiplying by ab, and then substituting the value of a² + b²

4b - 9a = ab
Substituting a² + b² = 10 in place of (a^2+b^2)
Rearrange terms:
ab=4b-9a 
a(b-9)=4b
b=\frac{4b}{(a+1)}
Dividing numerator and denominator by 'b'

We get,
\frac{b}{b}=\frac{4}{(a+1)}

Simplifying:

1 = 4/(a+1)

Multiplying both sides by (a+1):

(a + 1) = 4

Subtracting 1 from both sides to solve for 'a':

a = 3

Now that we have the value of a, let's substitute it back into the equation a² + b² = 10 to find the value of b.

(3)² + (b)² = 10

Simplifying:

9 + b² = 10

Subtracting 9 from both sides to solve for b²:

b² = 1

Taking the square root of both sides, we get:

|b| = √1
|b|=1
 
The final answer is: $\boxed{1}$

* The asymptotes of this hyperbola are given by:

Now, you want the slope of these asymptotes to be 2. So we have:
\[\left| \frac{b}{a} \right| = 2\]

This means either b/a = 2 or -b/a = 2.

Let's take the first one: 
\[ \frac{b}{a} = 2 \]
From this, I can write an equation for the hyperbola:
\[ \frac{x^2}{a^2} - \frac{y^2}{2a^2} = 1 \]

{(x^2)/a^2} - {(y-h)^2}/(b^2-a^2) = 1

where 'a' and 'b' are the lengths of the semi-major and semi-minor axes, respectively.

In this case, we're given that the foci lie on the y-axis. This implies that the transverse axis is horizontal. So, the standard form for our hyperbola will be:

{(y^2)/a^2} - {(x-h)^2}/(b^2-a^2) = 1

We also know that the distance from the center (h,k) to each focus is 'c', which equals the value of 'b'. Therefore, in our case, c = b = √(5^2 + 3^2), which gives us:

c = √34


Now we can use this value of c to find a and b by using the equations c² = a² + b² (for hyperbolas centered at the origin) or c² = a² + b² (when the center is not at the origin).

In our problem, since we have already assumed that the hyperbola's foci lie on the y-axis, it means they must be placed symmetrically around the vertex with respect to that axis. Thus, the given equation for this vertical transverse axis would be:

After solving these equations we get:


a² = 17
b² = 34
